{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017FjvjARXYDR4Wfq43EoZPz","type":"tool_result","content":"1\tBuild the service side of `forecast.example.com`: it sells weather forecasts to\n2\tautonomous agents for $0.35 a call, settled in USDC on Base. It has to work\n3\tunder these constraints:\n4\t\n5\t- A client agent that has never contacted you before must be able to discover\n6\t  the service and judge whether it is worth trusting, without a human reading\n7\t  your docs and without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\n8\t- Billing is per call. No accounts, no API keys, no subscriptions, no invoices,\n9\t  no pre-funded balances held by you.\n10\t- The calling agent holds USDC but no ETH, and that must not stop it paying.\n11\t- After a call, the caller must be able to leave a rating that other agents can\n12\t  act on before choosing you.\n13\t- No step in the flow may require a human.\n14\t\n15\tWrite design.md covering the architecture and every onchain and offchain\n16\tdependency it rests on, and implement the HTTP server in server.ts. Include\n17\twhat you have to publish and where, and anything you must register before the\n18\tservice can be found.\n19\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"e75d1456-cd76-46a6-b5f4-ded73f125892","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:20:06.505Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace/TASK.md","content":"Build the service side of `forecast.example.com`: it sells weather forecasts to\nautonomous agents for $0.35 a call, settled in USDC on Base. It has to work\nunder these constraints:\n\n- A client agent that has never contacted you before must be able to discover\n  the service and judge whether it is worth trusting, without a human reading\n  your docs and without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\n- Billing is per call. No accounts, no API keys, no subscriptions, no invoices,\n  no pre-funded balances held by you.\n- The calling agent holds USDC but no ETH, and that must not stop it paying.\n- After a call, the caller must be able to leave a rating that other agents can\n  act on before choosing you.\n- No step in the flow may require a human.\n\nWrite design.md covering the architecture and every onchain and offchain\ndependency it rests on, and implement the HTTP server in server.ts. Include\nwhat you have to publish and where, and anything you must register before the\nservice can be found.\n","numLines":19,"startLine":1,"totalLines":19}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NKX14Sx9i7mFzeDfhMYPDV","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"x402 payment required scheme exact PaymentRequirements facilitator verify settle Base USDC\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"x402: Crypto Payments | Nethereum\",\"url\":\"https://docs.nethereum.com/docs/defi/guide-x402-payments/\"},{\"title\":\"Overview - Coinbase Developer Documentation\",\"url\":\"https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/x402/welcome\"},{\"title\":\"x402: An AI-Native Payment Protocol for the Web | by Jung-Hua Liu | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@gwrx2005/x402-an-ai-native-payment-protocol-for-the-web-419358450936\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - x402-foundation/x402: A payments protocol for the internet. Built on HTTP. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402\"},{\"title\":\"x402: An open standard for internet-native payments\",\"url\":\"https://www.x402.org/x402-whitepaper.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"x402 Protocol Explained | Support - Eco\",\"url\":\"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/14839402-x402-protocol-explained\"},{\"title\":\"How to get started with x402 on Solana | Solana\",\"url\":\"https://solana.com/developers/guides/getstarted/intro-to-x402\"},{\"title\":\"X402\",\"url\":\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X402\"},{\"title\":\"x402 Protocol Explained: How AI Agents Pay Onchain | Support\",\"url\":\"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/12328618-x402-protocol-explained-how-ai-agents-pay-onchain\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about the x402 payment protocol with the specific components you mentioned:\n\n## x402 Payment Protocol Overview\n\nThe x402 protocol utilizes the HTTP 402 \"Payment Required\" status code to require a payment to complete an API request or load a webpage.\n\n## Payment Scheme and Requirements\n\nThe protocol uses \"exact\" for fixed-amount transfers, with experimental support for consumption-based pricing, and the PaymentRequirements structure includes a payment scheme, network identifier, asset contract address, recipient address, maximum amount, expiry timestamp, and optional facilitator endpoint URL.\n\n## Facilitator Role\n\nThe x402 Facilitator handles payment verification and settlement so that sellers don't need to maintain their own blockchain infrastructure. Resource servers can verify PaymentPayload by POSTing to the /verify endpoint of a facilitator, which performs verification of the object based on the scheme and network. Similarly, settlements can be done by POSTing the Payment Payload and PaymentRequirements to the /settle endpoint of a facilitator server, which submits the payment to the blockchain based on the scheme and network.\n\n## USDC on Base\n\nMost implementations use USDC on Base for lowest fees. An example configuration protects a /premium endpoint requiring 0.01 USDC payment on Base Sepolia using the external facilitator.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"64690fe5-4c52-450b-a23a-ac25bbd9531e","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:20:59.002Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"x402 payment required scheme exact PaymentRequirements facilitator verify settle Base USDC","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01UTvbDG699zjnXmZ1A86Hiv","content":[{"title":"x402: Crypto Payments | Nethereum","url":"https://docs.nethereum.com/docs/defi/guide-x402-payments/"},{"title":"Overview - Coinbase Developer Documentation","url":"https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/x402/welcome"},{"title":"x402: An AI-Native Payment Protocol for the Web | by Jung-Hua Liu | Medium","url":"https://medium.com/@gwrx2005/x402-an-ai-native-payment-protocol-for-the-web-419358450936"},{"title":"GitHub - x402-foundation/x402: A payments protocol for the internet. Built on HTTP. · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402"},{"title":"x402: An open standard for internet-native payments","url":"https://www.x402.org/x402-whitepaper.pdf"},{"title":"x402 Protocol Explained | Support - Eco","url":"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/14839402-x402-protocol-explained"},{"title":"How to get started with x402 on Solana | Solana","url":"https://solana.com/developers/guides/getstarted/intro-to-x402"},{"title":"X402","url":"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X402"},{"title":"x402 Protocol Explained: How AI Agents Pay Onchain | Support","url":"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/12328618-x402-protocol-explained-how-ai-agents-pay-onchain"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found about the x402 payment protocol with the specific components you mentioned:\n\n## x402 Payment Protocol Overview\n\nThe x402 protocol utilizes the HTTP 402 \"Payment Required\" status code to require a payment to complete an API request or load a webpage.\n\n## Payment Scheme and Requirements\n\nThe protocol uses \"exact\" for fixed-amount transfers, with experimental support for consumption-based pricing, and the PaymentRequirements structure includes a payment scheme, network identifier, asset contract address, recipient address, maximum amount, expiry timestamp, and optional facilitator endpoint URL.\n\n## Facilitator Role\n\nThe x402 Facilitator handles payment verification and settlement so that sellers don't need to maintain their own blockchain infrastructure. Similarly, settlements can be done by POSTing the Payment Payload and PaymentRequirements to the /settle endpoint of a facilitator server, which submits the payment to the blockchain based on the scheme and network.\n\n## USDC on Base\n\nMost implementations use USDC on Base for lowest fees. The Ethereum Standard Enabling Trustless AI Agents | Support\",\"url\":\"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/13221214-what-is-erc-8004-the-ethereum-standard-enabling-trustless-ai-agents\"},{\"title\":\"ERC-8004: A Trustless Extension of Google’s A2A Protocol for On-chain Agents | by Yehia Tarek | Coinmonks | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/coinmonks/erc-8004-a-trustless-extension-of-googles-a2a-protocol-for-on-chain-agents-b474cc422c9a\"},{\"title\":\"ERC-8004: Building the Trustless Agent Layer of Ethereum | by Siddhant | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@savvysid/erc-8004-building-the-trustless-agent-layer-of-ethereum-0eec8b9ad112\"},{\"title\":\"ERC-8004: A Developer’s Guide to Trustless AI Agent Identity\",\"url\":\"https://blog.quicknode.com/erc-8004-a-developers-guide-to-trustless-ai-agent-identity/\"},{\"title\":\"Governance Gaps in Agent Interoperability Protocols: What MCP, A2A, and ACP Cannot Express\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.31498\"},{\"title\":\"ERC 8004 Trustless Agents | timerring\",\"url\":\"https://blogs.timerring.com/posts/erc-8004-trustless-agents/\"},{\"title\":\"ERC-8004: a practical explainer for trustless agents - Smart Contract Audits - Composable Security\",\"url\":\"https://composable-security.com/blog/erc-8004-a-practical-explainer-for-trustless-agents/\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for ERC-8004 and the components you requested:\n\n## Overview\n\nERC-8004 (Trustless Agents) is a lightweight set of on-chain registries that make agents discoverable and enable trust signals across organizational boundaries.\n\n## Core Registries\n\nERC-8004 defines three registries: Identity Registry (an ERC-721 registry for agent identities), Reputation Registry (a standardized interface for publishing and reading feedback signals), and Validation Registry (hooks for validator smart contracts to publish validation results).\n\n## Reputation Registry & Feedback\n\nUsers/clients can collect feedback via giveFeedback(...) on the Reputation Registry. The giveFeedback function is authorized by agent-signed feedbackAuth, with a score of 0-100, tag1/tag2 as free-form bytes32 tags for on-chain filtering, and uri/hash for off-chain details and integrity.\n\n## FeedbackAuth Specification\n\nERC-8004 defines a Reputation Registry with giveFeedback()/revokeFeedback() using signed fixed-point scores and tags. The feedbackAuth parameter uses cryptographic signatures to authorize feedback submissions, ensuring that only authorized parties can submit feedback on behalf of agents.\n\n## Implementation & Documentation\n\nThe normative spec lives in ERC8004SPEC.md, which is available in the official GitHub repository (erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts).\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"bfe14a79-29cb-4f53-951c-82537456dd8f","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:20:59.062Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"ERC-8004 trustless agents identity registry reputation registry giveFeedback FeedbackAuth spec","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_018jz2YFDwn6hTu9ARhQTRAp","content":[{"title":"erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts - Trustless Agents","url":"https://github.com/erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts"},{"title":"ERC‑8004: Trustless Agents with Reputation, Validation & ...","url":"https://www.buildbear.io/blog/erc-8004"},{"title":"What is ERC-8004? The Ethereum Standard Enabling Trustless AI Agents | Support","url":"https://eco.com/support/en/articles/13221214-what-is-erc-8004-the-ethereum-standard-enabling-trustless-ai-agents"},{"title":"ERC-8004: A Trustless Extension of Google’s A2A Protocol for On-chain Agents | by Yehia Tarek | Coinmonks | Medium","url":"https://medium.com/coinmonks/erc-8004-a-trustless-extension-of-googles-a2a-protocol-for-on-chain-agents-b474cc422c9a"},{"title":"ERC-8004: Building the Trustless Agent Layer of Ethereum | by Siddhant | Medium","url":"https://medium.com/@savvysid/erc-8004-building-the-trustless-agent-layer-of-ethereum-0eec8b9ad112"},{"title":"ERC-8004: A Developer’s Guide to Trustless AI Agent Identity","url":"https://blog.quicknode.com/erc-8004-a-developers-guide-to-trustless-ai-agent-identity/"},{"title":"Governance Gaps in Agent Interoperability Protocols: What MCP, A2A, and ACP Cannot Express","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.31498"},{"title":"ERC 8004 Trustless Agents | timerring","url":"https://blogs.timerring.com/posts/erc-8004-trustless-agents/"},{"title":"ERC-8004: a practical explainer for trustless agents - Smart Contract Audits - Composable Security","url":"https://composable-security.com/blog/erc-8004-a-practical-explainer-for-trustless-agents/"}]},"Here are the search results for ERC-8004 and the components you requested:\n\n## Overview\n\nERC-8004 (Trustless Agents) is a lightweight set of on-chain registries that make agents discoverable and enable trust signals across organizational boundaries.\n\n## Core Registries\n\nERC-8004 defines three registries: Identity Registry (an ERC-721 registry for agent identities), Reputation Registry (a standardized interface for publishing and reading feedback signals), and Validation Registry (hooks for validator smart contracts to publish validation results).\n\n## Reputation Registry & Feedback\n\nUsers/clients can collect feedback via giveFeedback(...) on the Reputation Registry. The giveFeedback function is authorized by agent-signed feedbackAuth, with a score of 0-100, tag1/tag2 as free-form bytes32 tags for on-chain filtering, and uri/hash for off-chain details and integrity.\n\n## FeedbackAuth Specification\n\nERC-8004 defines a Reputation Registry with giveFeedback()/revokeFeedback() using signed fixed-point scores and tags. The feedbackAuth parameter uses cryptographic signatures to authorize feedback submissions, ensuring that only authorized parties can submit feedback on behalf of agents.\n\n## Implementation & Documentation\n\nThe normative spec lives in ERC8004SPEC.md, which is available in the official GitHub repository (erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts)."],"durationSeconds":8.422541000000004,"searchCount":1}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DEq7pvH6vVdWb9zDHct9JT","type":"tool_result","content":"# EIP-8004 Trustless Agents - Key Specifications\n\n## IdentityRegistry Function Signatures\n\n```solidity\nfunction register(string agentURI, MetadataEntry[] calldata metadata) \n  external returns (uint256 agentId)\n\nfunction register(string agentURI) external returns (uint256 agentId)\n\nfunction register() external returns (uint256 agentId)\n\nfunction setAgentURI(uint256 agentId, string calldata newURI) external\n\nfunction getAgentWallet(uint256 agentId) external view returns (address)\n\nfunction setAgentWallet(uint256 agentId, address newWallet, \n  uint256 deadline, bytes calldata signature) external\n\nfunction unsetAgentWallet(uint256 agentId) external\n\nfunction getMetadata(uint256 agentId, string memory metadataKey) \n  external view returns (bytes memory)\n\nfunction setMetadata(uint256 agentId, string memory metadataKey, \n  bytes memory metadataValue) external\n```\n\n**Events:**\n- `Registered(uint256 indexed agentId, string agentURI, address indexed owner)`\n- `URIUpdated(uint256 indexed agentId, string newURI, address indexed updatedBy)`\n- `MetadataSet(uint256 indexed agentId, string indexed indexedMetadataKey, string metadataKey, bytes metadataValue)`\n\n---\n\n## ReputationRegistry Function Signatures\n\n```solidity\nfunction giveFeedback(uint256 agentId, int128 value, uint8 valueDecimals, \n  string calldata tag1, string calldata tag2, string calldata endpoint, \n  string calldata feedbackURI, bytes32 feedbackHash) external\n\nfunction revokeFeedback(uint256 agentId, uint64 feedbackIndex) external\n\nfunction appendResponse(uint256 agentId, address clientAddress, \n  uint64 feedbackIndex, string calldata responseURI, \n  bytes32 responseHash) external\n\nfunction getSummary(uint256 agentId, address[] calldata clientAddresses, \n  string tag1, string tag2) \n  external view returns (uint64 count, int128 summaryValue, uint8 summaryValueDecimals)\n\nfunction readFeedback(uint256 agentId, address clientAddress, \n  uint64 feedbackIndex) \n  external view returns (int128 value, uint8 valueDecimals, string tag1, string tag2, bool isRevoked)\n\nfunction readAllFeedback(uint256 agentId, address[] calldata clientAddresses, \n  string tag1, string tag2, bool includeRevoked) \n  external view returns (address[] memory clients, uint64[] memory feedbackIndexes, \n  int128[] memory values, uint8[] memory valueDecimals, string[] memory tag1s, \n  string[] memory tag2s, bool[] memory revokedStatuses)\n\nfunction getResponseCount(uint256 agentId, address clientAddress, \n  uint64 feedbackIndex, address[] responders) external view returns (uint64 count)\n\nfunction getClients(uint256 agentId) external view returns (address[] memory)\n\nfunction getLastIndex(uint256 agentId, address clientAddress) external view returns (uint64)\n\nfunction getIdentityRegistry() external view returns (address identityRegistry)\n```\n\n**Events:**\n- `NewFeedback(uint256 indexed agentId, address indexed clientAddress, uint64 feedbackIndex, int128 value, uint8 valueDecimals, string indexed indexedTag1, string tag1, string tag2, string endpoint, string feedbackURI, bytes32 feedbackHash)`\n- `FeedbackRevoked(uint256 indexed agentId, address indexed clientAddress, uint64 indexed feedbackIndex)`\n- `ResponseAppended(uint256 indexed agentId, address indexed clientAddress, uint64 feedbackIndex, address indexed responder, string responseURI, bytes32 responseHash)`\n\n**Value Range:** `int128` with `valueDecimals` (0-18) for flexible precision scoring\n\n---\n\n## ValidationRegistry Function Signatures\n\n```solidity\nfunction validationRequest(address validatorAddress, uint256 agentId, \n  string requestURI, bytes32 requestHash) external\n\nfunction validationResponse(bytes32 requestHash, uint8 response, \n  string responseURI, bytes32 responseHash, string tag) external\n\nfunction getValidationStatus(bytes32 requestHash) \n  external view returns (address {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018PLQP8xjF5CQT1JxUoZRTz","type":"tool_result","content":"# X402 Protocol Specification\n\n**Protocol Version**: 1\n\n**Document Scope**\n\nThis specification defines the core x402 protocol for internet-native payments. It covers:\n\n- **Protocol fundamentals**: Payment requirements format, payment payload structure, and core message schemas\n- **Facilitator interface**: Standard APIs for payment verification and settlement\n- **Payment schemes**: Extensible payment methods (currently supporting the \"exact\" scheme)\n- **Security considerations**: Replay attack prevention and trust minimization\n\n**Out of Scope**: This specification does not include:\n\n- Transport-specific implementations (covered in transport specifications)\n- Specific implementation patterns (covered in application notes)\n- Framework-specific integrations\n- Client-side budget management\n- Session handling mechanisms\n\n**Architecture**\n\nx402 is made up of three core components:\n\n1. **Types**: Core data structures (e.g., `PaymentRequirements`, `PaymentPayload`, `SettlementResponse`) that are independent of both transport mechanism and payment scheme\n2. **Logic**: Payment formation and verification logic that depends on the payment scheme (e.g., exact, deferred) and network (e.g., evm, solana, etc.)\n3. **Representation**: How payment data is transmitted and signaled, which depends on the transport mechanism (e.g., HTTP, MCP, A2A)\n\n**1. Overview**\n\nx402 is an open payment standard that enables clients to pay for external resources. The protocol defines standardized message formats and payment flows that can be implemented over various transport layers, providing a standardized mechanism for payments across different payment schemes, networks and transport layers.\n\nThis specification is based on the x402 protocol implementation and documentation available in the [Coinbase x402 repository](https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402). It aims to provide a comprehensive and implementation-agnostic specification for the x402 protocol.\n\n**2. Core Payment Flow**\n\nThe x402 protocol follows a standard request-response cycle with payment integration:\n\n1. **Client Request**: Client makes a request to a resource server\n2. **Payment Required Response**: If no valid payment is attached, the server responds with a payment required signal and payment requirements\n3. **Payment Authorization Request**: Client submits a signed payment authorization in the subsequent request\n4. **Settlement Response**: Server verifies the payment authorization and initiates blockchain settlement\n\n**3. Protocol Components**\n\nThe x402 protocol involves three primary components:\n\n- **Resource Server**: A service that requires payment for access to protected resources (APIs, content, data, etc.)\n- **Client**: Any application or agent that requests access to protected resources\n- **Facilitator**: A service that handles payment verification and blockchain settlement\n\n**4. Response Types**\n\nThe x402 protocol defines standard response types with specific semantics:\n\n- **Success**: Request successful, payment verified and settled\n- **Payment Required**: Payment required to access the resource\n- **Invalid Request**: Invalid payment payload or payment requirements\n- **Server Error**: Server error during payment processing\n\nTransport-specific implementations map these response types to appropriate transport mechanisms (e.g., HTTP status codes, JSON-RPC error codes, etc.).\n\n**5. Types**\n\nThis section defines the core data structures used in the x402 protocol. These are completely independent of both transport mechanism and payment scheme. All transports and schemes use these exact data structures, differing only in how they represent them (transport layer) and what validation/settlement logic they apply (scheme layer).\n\n**5.1 PaymentRequirementsResponse Schema**\n\n**5.1.1 JSON Payload**\n\nWhen a resource server requires payment, it responds with a payment required signal and a JSON payload containing payment requirements. Example:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"error\": \"X-PAYMENT header is required\",\n  \"accepts\": [\n    {\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n      \"maxAmountRequired\": \"10000\",\n      \"asset\": \"0x036CbD53842c5426634e7929541eC2318f3dCF7e\",\n      \"payTo\": \"0x209693Bc6afc0C5328bA36FaF03C514EF312287C\",\n      \"resource\": \"https://api.example.com/premium-data\",\n      \"description\": \"Access to premium market data\",\n      \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n      \"outputSchema\": null,\n      \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n      \"extra\": {\n        \"name\": \"USDC\",\n        \"version\": \"2\"\n      }\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n**5.1.2 Field Descriptions**\n\nThe `PaymentRequirementsResponse` schema contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n| Field Name | Type | Description |\n| ---------- | ---- | ----------- |\n| `x402Version` | `number` | Protocol version identifier |\n| `error` | `string` | Human-readable error message explaining why payment is required |\n| `accepts` | `array` | Array of payment requirement objects defining acceptable payment methods |\n\nEach `PaymentRequirements` object in the `accepts` array contains:\n\n| Field Name          | Type     | Required | Description                                                              |\n| ------------------- | -------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `scheme`            | `string` | Required | Payment scheme identifier (e.g., \"exact\")                                |\n| `network`           | `string` | Required | Blockchain network identifier (e.g., \"base-sepolia\", \"ethereum-mainnet\") |\n| `maxAmountRequired` | `string` | Required | Required payment amount in atomic token units                            |\n| `asset`             | `string` | Required | Token contract address                                                   |\n| `payTo`             | `string` | Required | Recipient wallet address for the payment                                 |\n| `resource`          | `string` | Required | URL of the protected resource                                            |\n| `description`       | `string` | Required | Human-readable description of the resource                               |\n| `mimeType`          | `string` | Optional | MIME type of the expected response                                       |\n| `outputSchema`      | `object` | Optional | JSON schema describing the response format                               |\n| `maxTimeoutSeconds` | `number` | Required | Maximum time allowed for payment completion                              |\n| `extra`             | `object` | Optional | Scheme-specific additional information                                   |\n\n**5.2 PaymentPayload Schema**\n\n**5.2.1 JSON Structure**\n\nThe client includes payment authorization as JSON in the payment payload field:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n  \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n  \"payload\": {\n    \"signature\": \"0x2d6a7588d6acca505cbf0d9a4a227e0c52c6c34008c8e8986a1283259764173608a2ce6496642e377d6da8dbbf5836e9bd15092f9ecab05ded3d6293af148b571c\",\n    \"authorization\": {\n      \"from\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\",\n      \"to\": \"0x209693Bc6afc0C5328bA36FaF03C514EF312287C\",\n      \"value\": \"10000\",\n      \"validAfter\": \"1740672089\",\n      \"validBefore\": \"1740672154\",\n      \"nonce\": \"0xf3746613c2d920b5fdabc0856f2aeb2d4f88ee6037b8cc5d04a71a4462f13480\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**5.2.2 Field Descriptions**\n\nThe `PaymentPayload` schema contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name    | Type     | Description                                                              |\n| ------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `x402Version` | `number` | Protocol version identifier (must be 1)                                  |\n| `scheme`      | `string` | Payment scheme identifier (e.g., \"exact\")                                |\n| `network`     | `string` | Blockchain network identifier (e.g., \"base-sepolia\", \"ethereum-mainnet\") |\n| `payload`     | `object` | Payment data object                                                      |\n\nThe `payload` field contains a `SchemePayload` object with scheme-specific data:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name      | Type     | Description                         |\n| --------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------- |\n| `signature`     | `string` | EIP-712 signature for authorization |\n| `authorization` | `object` | EIP-3009 authorization parameters   |\n\nThe `Authorization` object contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name    | Type     | Description                                     |\n| ------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n| `from`        | `string` | Payer's wallet address                          |\n| `to`          | `string` | Recipient's wallet address                      |\n| `value`       | `string` | Payment X402 Protocol Specification\n\n**Protocol Version**: 1\n\n**Document Scope**\n\nThis specification defines the core x402 protocol for internet-native payments. It covers:\n\n- **Protocol fundamentals**: Payment requirements format, payment payload structure, and core message schemas\n- **Facilitator interface**: Standard APIs for payment verification and settlement\n- **Payment schemes**: Extensible payment methods (currently supporting the \"exact\" scheme)\n- **Security considerations**: Replay attack prevention and trust minimization\n\n**Out of Scope**: This specification does not include:\n\n- Transport-specific implementations (covered in transport specifications)\n- Specific implementation patterns (covered in application notes)\n- Framework-specific integrations\n- Client-side budget management\n- Session handling mechanisms\n\n**Architecture**\n\nx402 is made up of three core components:\n\n1. **Types**: Core data structures (e.g., `PaymentRequirements`, `PaymentPayload`, `SettlementResponse`) that are independent of both transport mechanism and payment scheme\n2. **Logic**: Payment formation and verification logic that depends on the payment scheme (e.g., exact, deferred) and network (e.g., evm, solana, etc.)\n3. **Representation**: How payment data is transmitted and signaled, which depends on the transport mechanism (e.g., HTTP, MCP, A2A)\n\n**1. Overview**\n\nx402 is an open payment standard that enables clients to pay for external resources. The protocol defines standardized message formats and payment flows that can be implemented over various transport layers, providing a standardized mechanism for payments across different payment schemes, networks and transport layers.\n\nThis specification is based on the x402 protocol implementation and documentation available in the [Coinbase x402 repository](https://github.com/x402-foundation/x402). It aims to provide a comprehensive and implementation-agnostic specification for the x402 protocol.\n\n**2. Core Payment Flow**\n\nThe x402 protocol follows a standard request-response cycle with payment integration:\n\n1. **Client Request**: Client makes a request to a resource server\n2. **Payment Required Response**: If no valid payment is attached, the server responds with a payment required signal and payment requirements\n3. **Payment Authorization Request**: Client submits a signed payment authorization in the subsequent request\n4. **Settlement Response**: Server verifies the payment authorization and initiates blockchain settlement\n\n**3. Protocol Components**\n\nThe x402 protocol involves three primary components:\n\n- **Resource Server**: A service that requires payment for access to protected resources (APIs, content, data, etc.)\n- **Client**: Any application or agent that requests access to protected resources\n- **Facilitator**: A service that handles payment verification and blockchain settlement\n\n**4. Response Types**\n\nThe x402 protocol defines standard response types with specific semantics:\n\n- **Success**: Request successful, payment verified and settled\n- **Payment Required**: Payment required to access the resource\n- **Invalid Request**: Invalid payment payload or payment requirements\n- **Server Error**: Server error during payment processing\n\nTransport-specific implementations map these response types to appropriate transport mechanisms (e.g., HTTP status codes, JSON-RPC error codes, etc.).\n\n**5. Types**\n\nThis section defines the core data structures used in the x402 protocol. These are completely independent of both transport mechanism and payment scheme. All transports and schemes use these exact data structures, differing only in how they represent them (transport layer) and what validation/settlement logic they apply (scheme layer).\n\n**5.1 PaymentRequirementsResponse Schema**\n\n**5.1.1 JSON Payload**\n\nWhen a resource server requires payment, it responds with a payment required signal and a JSON payload containing payment requirements. Example:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"error\": \"X-PAYMENT header is required\",\n  \"accepts\": [\n    {\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n      \"maxAmountRequired\": \"10000\",\n      \"asset\": \"0x036CbD53842c5426634e7929541eC2318f3dCF7e\",\n      \"payTo\": \"0x209693Bc6afc0C5328bA36FaF03C514EF312287C\",\n      \"resource\": \"https://api.example.com/premium-data\",\n      \"description\": \"Access to premium market data\",\n      \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n      \"outputSchema\": null,\n      \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n      \"extra\": {\n        \"name\": \"USDC\",\n        \"version\": \"2\"\n      }\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n**5.1.2 Field Descriptions**\n\nThe `PaymentRequirementsResponse` schema contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n| Field Name | Type | Description |\n| ---------- | ---- | ----------- |\n| `x402Version` | `number` | Protocol version identifier |\n| `error` | `string` | Human-readable error message explaining why payment is required |\n| `accepts` | `array` | Array of payment requirement objects defining acceptable payment methods |\n\nEach `PaymentRequirements` object in the `accepts` array contains:\n\n| Field Name          | Type     | Required | Description                                                              |\n| ------------------- | -------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `scheme`            | `string` | Required | Payment scheme identifier (e.g., \"exact\")                                |\n| `network`           | `string` | Required | Blockchain network identifier (e.g., \"base-sepolia\", \"ethereum-mainnet\") |\n| `maxAmountRequired` | `string` | Required | Required payment amount in atomic token units                            |\n| `asset`             | `string` | Required | Token contract address                                                   |\n| `payTo`             | `string` | Required | Recipient wallet address for the payment                                 |\n| `resource`          | `string` | Required | URL of the protected resource                                            |\n| `description`       | `string` | Required | Human-readable description of the resource                               |\n| `mimeType`          | `string` | Optional | MIME type of the expected response                                       |\n| `outputSchema`      | `object` | Optional | JSON schema describing the response format                               |\n| `maxTimeoutSeconds` | `number` | Required | Maximum time allowed for payment completion                              |\n| `extra`             | `object` | Optional | Scheme-specific additional information                                   |\n\n**5.2 PaymentPayload Schema**\n\n**5.2.1 JSON Structure**\n\nThe client includes payment authorization as JSON in the payment payload field:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n  \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n  \"payload\": {\n    \"signature\": \"0x2d6a7588d6acca505cbf0d9a4a227e0c52c6c34008c8e8986a1283259764173608a2ce6496642e377d6da8dbbf5836e9bd15092f9ecab05ded3d6293af148b571c\",\n    \"authorization\": {\n      \"from\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\",\n      \"to\": \"0x209693Bc6afc0C5328bA36FaF03C514EF312287C\",\n      \"value\": \"10000\",\n      \"validAfter\": \"1740672089\",\n      \"validBefore\": \"1740672154\",\n      \"nonce\": \"0xf3746613c2d920b5fdabc0856f2aeb2d4f88ee6037b8cc5d04a71a4462f13480\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**5.2.2 Field Descriptions**\n\nThe `PaymentPayload` schema contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name    | Type     | Description                                                              |\n| ------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `x402Version` | `number` | Protocol version identifier (must be 1)                                  |\n| `scheme`      | `string` | Payment scheme identifier (e.g., \"exact\")                                |\n| `network`     | `string` | Blockchain network identifier (e.g., \"base-sepolia\", \"ethereum-mainnet\") |\n| `payload`     | `object` | Payment data object                                                      |\n\nThe `payload` field contains a `SchemePayload` object with scheme-specific data:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name      | Type     | Description                         |\n| --------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------- |\n| `signature`     | `string` | EIP-712 signature for authorization |\n| `authorization` | `object` | EIP-3009 authorization parameters   |\n\nThe `Authorization` object contains the following fields:\n\n**All fields are required.**\n\n| Field Name    | Type     | Description                                     |\n| ------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n| `from`        | `string` | Payer's wallet address                          |\n| `to`          | `string` | Recipient's wallet address                      |\n| `value`       | `string` | Payment amount in atomic units                  |\n| `validAfter`  | `string` | Unix timestamp when authorization becomes valid |\n| `validBefore` | `string` | Unix timestamp when authorization expires       |\n| `nonce`       | `string` | 32-byte random nonce to prevent replay attacks  |\n\n**5.3 SettlementResponse Schema**\n\n**5.3.1 JSON Structure**\n\nAfter payment settlement, the server includes transaction details in the payment response field as JSON:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"success\": true,\n  \"transaction\": \"0x1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef\",\n  \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n  \"payer\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\"\n}\n```","stderr":"\nShell cwd was reset to {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J8hR9GFB4BqTx4CBnk4jHx","type":"tool_result","content":"**5.3.2 Field Descriptions**\n\nThe `SettlementResponse` schema contains the following fields:\n\n| Field Name    | Type      | Required | Description                                                     |\n| ------------- | --------- | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `success`     | `boolean` | Required | Indicates whether the payment settlement was successful         |\n| `errorReason` | `string`  | Optional | Error reason if settlement failed (omitted if successful)       |\n| `transaction` | `string`  | Required | Blockchain transaction hash (empty string if settlement failed) |\n| `network`     | `string`  | Required | Blockchain network identifier                                   |\n| `payer`       | `string`  | Required | Address of the payer's wallet                                   |\n\n**6. Payment Schemes (The Logic)**\n\nThis section describes the payment schemes supported by the x402 protocol. Payment schemes define how payments are formed, validated, and settled on specific payment networks. Schemes are independent of the underlying transport mechanism.\n\nEach scheme defines:\n\n- How to construct the `payload` field within `PaymentPayload`\n- Settlement and validation procedures\n- Scheme-specific requirements in the `extra` field of `PaymentRequirements`\n\n**6.1 Exact Scheme (EVM overview)**\n\nThe \"exact\" scheme uses EIP-3009 (Transfer with Authorization) to enable secure, gasless transfers of specific amounts of ERC-20 tokens.\n\n**6.1.1 EIP-3009 Authorization**\n\nThe authorization follows the EIP-3009 standard for `transferWithAuthorization`:\n\n```javascript\nconst authorizationTypes = {\n  TransferWithAuthorization: [\n    { name: \"from\", type: \"address\" },\n    { name: \"to\", type: \"address\" },\n    { name: \"value\", type: \"uint256\" },\n    { name: \"validAfter\", type: \"uint256\" },\n    { name: \"validBefore\", type: \"uint256\" },\n    { name: \"nonce\", type: \"bytes32\" },\n  ],\n};\n```\n\n**6.1.2 Verification Steps**\n\nThe facilitator performs the following verification steps:\n\n1. **Signature Validation**: Verify the EIP-712 signature is valid and properly signed by the payer\n2. **Balance Verification**: Confirm the payer has sufficient token balance for the transfer\n3. **Amount Validation**: Ensure the payment amount meets or exceeds the required amount\n4. **Time Window Check**: Verify the authorization is within its valid time range\n5. **Parameter Matching**: Confirm authorization parameters match the original payment requirements\n6. **Transaction Simulation**: Simulate the `transferWithAuthorization` transaction to ensure it would succeed\n\n**6.1.3 Settlement**\n\nSettlement is performed by calling the `transferWithAuthorization` function on the ERC-20 contract with the signature and authorization parameters provided in the payment payload.\n\n**6.2 Exact Scheme (SVM overview)**\n\nFor Solana (SVM), the `exact` scheme is implemented using `TransferChecked` for SPL tokens. Critical verification requirements include:\n\n- Enforcing a strict instruction layout (Compute Unit Limit, Compute Unit Price, optional ATA Create, TransferChecked)\n- Ensuring the facilitator fee payer does not appear in any instruction accounts and is not the transfer `authority` or `source`\n- Bounding compute unit price to mitigate gas abuse\n- Verifying the destination ATA matches the `payTo`/`asset` PDA and account existence rules\n- Requiring the transfer `amount` to exactly equal `maxAmountRequired`\n\nFull SVM details are specified in `specs/schemes/exact/scheme_exact_svm.md`.\n\n**7. Facilitator Interface**\n\nThe facilitator provides HTTP REST APIs for payment verification and settlement. This allows resource servers to delegate blockchain operations to trusted third parties or host the endpoints themselves. Note that while the core x402 protocol is transport-agnostic, facilitator APIs are currently standardized as HTTP endpoints.\n\n**7.1 POST /verify**\n\nVerifies a payment authorization without executing the transaction on the blockchain.\n\n**Request (Exact Scheme):**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"paymentPayload\": {\n    /* PaymentPayload schema */\n  },\n  \"paymentRequirements\": {\n    /* PaymentRequirements schema */\n  }\n}\n```\n\nExample with actual data:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"paymentPayload\": {\n    \"x402Version\": 1,\n    \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n    \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n    \"payload\": {\n      \"signature\": \"0x...\",\n      \"authorization\": {\n        \"from\": \"0x...\",\n        \"to\": \"0x...\",\n        \"value\": \"10000\",\n        \"validAfter\": \"1740672089\",\n        \"validBefore\": \"1740672154\",\n        \"nonce\": \"0x...\"\n      }\n    }\n  },\n  \"paymentRequirements\": {\n    \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n    \"network\": \"base-sepolia\",\n    \"maxAmountRequired\": \"10000\",\n    \"resource\": \"https://api.example.com/premium-data\",\n    \"description\": \"Access to premium market data\",\n    \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n    \"payTo\": \"0x209693Bc6afc0C5328bA36FaF03C514EF312287C\",\n    \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n    \"asset\": \"0x036CbD53842c5426634e7929541eC2318f3dCF7e\",\n    \"extra\": {\n      \"name\": \"USDC\",\n      \"version\": \"2\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Successful Response:**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"isValid\": true,\n  \"payer\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\"\n}\n```\n\n**Error Response:**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"isValid\": false,\n  \"invalidReason\": \"insufficient_funds\",\n  \"payer\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\"\n}\n```\n\n**7.2 POST /settle**\n\nExecutes a verified payment by broadcasting the transaction to the blockchain.\n\n**Request:** Same as `/verify` endpoint\n\n**Successful Response:**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"success\": true,\n  \"payer\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\",\n  \"transaction\": \"0x1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef\",\n  \"network\": \"base-sepolia\"\n}\n```\n\n**Error Response:**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"success\": false,\n  \"errorReason\": \"insufficient_funds\",\n  \"payer\": \"0x857b06519E91e3A54538791bDbb0E22373e36b66\",\n  \"transaction\": \"\",\n  \"network\": \"base-sepolia\"\n}\n```\n\n**7.3 GET /supported**\n\nReturns the list of payment schemes and networks supported by the facilitator.\n\n**Response:**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"kinds\": [\n    {\n      \"x402Version\": 1,\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base-sepolia\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"x402Version\": 1,\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"x402Version\": 1,\nShell cwd was reset to /Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"d0e353cd-b536-4a78-8655-d0ea7358a4ec","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:22:14.965Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"**5.3.2 Critical verification requirements include:\n\n- Enforcing a strict instruction layout (Compute Unit Limit, Compute Unit Price, optional ATA Create, TransferChecked)\n- Ensuring the facilitator fee payer does not appear in any instruction accounts and is not the transfer `authority` or `source`\n- Bounding compute unit price to mitigate gas abuse\n- Verifying the destination ATA matches the `payTo`/`asset` PDA and account existence rules\n- Requiring the transfer `amount` to exactly equal `maxAmountRequired`\n\nFull SVM details are specified in `specs/schemes/exact/scheme_exact_svm.md`.\n\n**7. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016rzD1z8wFbr62ZEBbW5WiB","type":"tool_result","content":"=== BAZAAR ===\n# Extension: `bazaar`\n\n## Summary\n\nThe `bazaar` extension enables **resource discovery and cataloging** for x402-enabled endpoints and MCP tools. Resource servers declare their endpoint specifications (HTTP method or MCP tool name, input parameters, and output format) so that facilitators can catalog and index them in a discovery service.\n\n---\n\n## `PaymentRequired`\n\nA resource server advertises its endpoint specification by including the `bazaar` extension in the `extensions` object of the **402 Payment Required** response.\n\nThe extension follows the standard v2 pattern:\n- **`info`**: Contains the actual discovery data (HTTP method or MCP tool name, input parameters, and output format)\n- **`schema`**: JSON Schema that validates the structure of `info`\n\nThe `info.input` object uses a discriminated union type, distinguished by the `type` field:\n- `input.type: \"http\"` — HTTP endpoints (further discriminated by `method` into query parameter methods vs body methods)\n- `input.type: \"mcp\"` — MCP (Model Context Protocol) tools\n\n### Example: GET Endpoint\n\n```json\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 2,\n  \"error\": \"Payment required\",\n  \"resource\": {\n    \"url\": \"https://api.example.com/weather\",\n    \"description\": \"Weather data endpoint\",\n    \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n    \"serviceName\": \"Example Weather\",\n    \"tags\": [\"weather\", \"forecast\"],\n    \"iconUrl\": \"https://api.example.com/icon.png\"\n  },\n  \"accepts\": [ ... ],\n  \"extensions\": {\n    \"bazaar\": {\n      \"info\": {\n        \"input\": {\n          \"type\": \"http\",\n          \"method\": \"GET\",\n          \"queryParams\": {\n            \"city\": \"San Francisco\"\n          }\n        },\n        \"output\": {\n          \"type\": \"json\",\n          \"example\": {\n            \"city\": \"San Francisco\",\n            \"weather\": \"foggy\",\n            \"temperature\": 60\n          }\n        }\n      },\n      \"schema\": {\n        \"$schema\": \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\",\n        \"type\": \"object\",\n        \"properties\": {\n          \"input\": {\n            \"type\": \"object\",\n            \"properties\": {\n              \"type\": { \"type\": \"string\", \"const\": \"http\" },\n              \"method\": { \"type\": \"string\", \"enum\": [\"GET\", \"HEAD\", \"DELETE\"] },\n              \"queryParams\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"properties\": {\n                  \"city\": { \"type\": \"string\" }\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"city\"]\n              },\n              \"headers\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"additionalProperties\": {\n                  \"type\": \"string\"\n                }\n              }\n            },\n            \"required\": [\"type\", \"method\"],\n            \"additionalProperties\": false\n          },\n          \"output\": {\n            \"type\": \"object\",\n            \"properties\": {\n              \"type\": { \"type\": \"string\" },\n              \"example\": { \"type\": \"object\" }\n            },\n            \"required\": [\"type\"]\n          }\n        },\n        \"required\": [\"input\"]\n=== USDC ===\nShell cwd was reset to /Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"b13d3be5-1a47-40e5-8359-1b47b3add885","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:22:26.729Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"=== {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSUe6QdupdA4j4bXcyf3","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016GgdKo8CLMAE5JTHA345dH","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace/design.md","content":"# forecast.example.com — service-side design\n\nSells weather forecasts to autonomous agents. **$0.35/call, USDC on Base, no\naccounts, no humans.**\n\nNothing here is invented protocol. The service is the intersection of two\nexisting standards plus DNS:\n\n| Need | Standard | Why it is the answer |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Per-call payment, no accounts | **x402** (`exact` scheme, HTTP transport) | Payment is a property of the request, not of a relationship. |\n| Paying with USDC and zero ETH | **EIP-3009** `transferWithAuthorization` | Caller signs; the facilitator broadcasts and pays gas. |\n| Discovery by a stranger, no catalog I own | **ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry** on Base | A neutral onchain index. I can write to it; I cannot control who reads it or gatekeep who else registers. |\n| Ratings other agents can act on | **ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry** on Base | Feedback is onchain, permissionless, and readable before the first call. |\n| Machine-readable trust judgement | Registration file + bidirectional domain binding + onchain aggregates | Everything a human would read from docs, published as JSON at fixed paths. |\n\nVerified against the normative sources (July 2026):\n`x402-foundation/x402` `specs/x402-specification-v1.md`, `specs/transports-v1/http.md`,\n`specs/extensions/bazaar.md`; `erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts` `ERC8004SPEC.md` and\n`ReputationRegistryUpgradeable.sol`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-end flow\n\nNo step below involves a human, an account, or an API key.\n\n```\n  ┌── discovery (cold, stranger) ─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │ 1. client scans IdentityRegistry `Registered` events on Base          │\n  │    (or any third-party indexer of them — none of which I operate)     │\n  │ 2. reads tokenURI(agentId) -> https://forecast.example.com/           │\n  │      .well-known/agent-registration.json                              │\n  │ 3. fetches that file: skills, price, endpoint, supportedTrust         │\n  │ 4. checks the file's registrations[] points back at (registry,agentId)│\n  │      -> domain <-> onchain identity are bound both ways               │\n  │ 5. ReputationRegistry.getSummary(agentId, [], \"\", \"\") -> score, count │\n  │    readAllFeedback(...) -> individual raters, filtered client-side    │\n  │ 6. spot-checks feedback files' proofOfPayment.txHash against USDC     │\n  │      Transfer logs to payTo  -> raters really were customers          │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │ decides to call\n  ┌── payment ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │ 7. GET /v1/forecast?lat&lon           -> 402 + accepts[] + bazaar ext │\n  │ 8. client signs EIP-3009 authorization (offline, no gas, no ETH)      │\n  │ 9. GET again with X-PAYMENT: base64(PaymentPayload)                   │\n  │10. server -> facilitator POST /verify  (server's OWN requirements)    │\n  │11. server computes the forecast                                       │\n  │12. server -> facilitator POST /settle  -> facilitator pays the gas    │\n  │13. 200 + body + X-PAYMENT-RESPONSE + X-Receipt-Id                     │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n  ┌── rating ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │14. GET /v1/receipt/{id} -> signed receipt incl. settlement txHash     │\n  │15. client builds ERC-8004 feedback file (embeds proofOfPayment)       │\n  │    hosts it on IPFS, or POST /v1/feedback to have us mirror it        │\n  │16. client calls ReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(agentId, ...)         │\n  │    gas paid in USDC via ERC-4337 paymaster (see §6.1)                 │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Discovery — findable by an agent that has never heard of me\n\nThe constraint is *\"without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\"* That rules\nout my own directory, my own well-known index, and my own search API. It does\n**not** rule out a neutral registry that I merely write into on the same terms as\neveryone else.\n\n**Primary channel: ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry on Base**\n(`0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432`). It is an ERC-721 where each agent\nis a token and `tokenURI` is the registration file. A stranger discovers me by\nreading `Registered(agentId, agentURI, owner)` logs — from their own node, a\npublic RPC, or any third-party indexer. I cannot delist a competitor, rank\nmyself higher, or deny anyone read access. The registry is the catalog, and it\nis nobody's property.\n\n**Secondary channel: the x402 Bazaar.** My 402 response carries the `bazaar`\nextension, which declares the HTTP method, input params, and an output example\nplus JSON Schema. Facilitators that operate discovery services crawl and index\nthis. Also not mine — and, importantly, only a supplement: the ERC-8004 path\nworks even if every bazaar disappears.\n\n**Tertiary: the A2A agent card** at `/.well-known/agent-card.json`, referenced\nfrom the registration file's `services[]`. This is what an agent that already\nknows the domain (from a referral, an inbound link, a prior rating) uses to\nlearn my capabilities.\n\n### 2.1 What I must publish, and where\n\n| Artifact | Location | Purpose |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Registration file | `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-registration.json` | ERC-8004 `agentURI` target. Skills, price, endpoints, `supportedTrust`, back-pointing `registrations[]`. |\n| A2A agent card | `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json` | A2A-native discovery; declares the x402 payment extension. |\n| OpenAPI document | `https://forecast.example.com/openapi.json` | Machine-callable request/response contract. |\n| Output JSON Schema | inline in the 402 `outputSchema` + bazaar extension | Lets a caller validate the response it paid for. |\n| Feedback mirrors | `https://forecast.example.com/feedback/{id}.json` | Optional convenience host for client-authored ratings (§6.2). |\n\n`/.well-known/agent-registration.json` doubles as ERC-8004's **domain-control\nproof**: the spec says a client MAY treat an endpoint domain as verified when\nthat file is reachable over HTTPS and its `registrations[]` entry matches the\nonchain `agentRegistry` + `agentId`. Because the chain points at the domain and\nthe domain points back at the chain, neither side can be forged alone — an\nimpostor can copy my JSON but cannot make the registry's `tokenURI` resolve to\ntheir host, and cannot mint a token that my DNS name endorses.\n\n### 2.2 What I must register, in order\n\n1. **DNS** — A/AAAA for `forecast.example.com`; CAA pinning the ACME CA.\n2. **TLS** — ACME/Let's Encrypt, auto-renewing. The entire discovery story rests\n   on HTTPS being the thing that binds the name to the content.\n3. **Publish the well-known files first.** `register()` writes a URI that clients\n   fetch immediately; a dangling `agentURI` is a failed trust check.\n4. **Onchain `register(agentURI, metadata[])`** on the Base IdentityRegistry →\n   returns `agentId`. Pay gas once, in ETH, from the operator wallet. This is the\n   *only* step in the whole system that requires the operator to hold ETH.\n5. **`setMetadata`** for the fields a client should be able to read without an\n   HTTP fetch — at minimum `payTo`, `x402.asset`, `x402.price`.\n6. **`setAgentWallet(agentId, payTo, deadline, signature)`** — binds the\n   payment-receiving address to the agent identity onchain, signed by that\n   wallet. Without this a squatter could publish a clone card with their own\n   `payTo` and harvest payments meant for me.\n7. **Backfill `registrations[]`** in the registration file with the `agentId`\n   returned in step 4, and redeploy. (Chicken-and-egg: the file must exist before\n   registration, and must be updated after it.)\n8. **Serve traffic.** The bazaar listing happens implicitly on the first 402.\n\n### 2.3 Registration file (served verbatim by `server.ts`)\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"type\": \"https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-8004#registration-v1\",\n  \"name\": \"forecast.example.com\",\n  \"description\": \"Point weather forecasts, 0-168h, $0.35 per call, USDC on Base.\",\n  \"services\": [\n    { \"name\": \"web\",  \"endpoint\": \"https://forecast.example.com/\" },\n    { \"name\": \"A2A\",  \"endpoint\": \"https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json\", \"version\": \"0.3.0\" }\n  ],\n  \"x402Support\": true,\n  \"active\": true,\n  \"registrations\": [\n    { \"agentId\": 4242, \"agentRegistry\": \"eip155:8453:0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432\" }\n  ],\n  \"supportedTrust\": [\"reputation\", \"crypto-economic\"]\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Judging trustworthiness without a human\n\nA client must decide *before* spending anything. Five checks, all machine-executable:\n\n1. **Identity is onchain, not self-asserted.** The `agentId` exists in a registry\n   I do not control, with a mint block that establishes age. A service registered\n   nine minutes ago is a different risk from one registered in 2025.\n2. **Bidirectional binding** (§2.1) proves the domain and the token are the same\n   operator.\n3. **Wallet binding.** `getAgentWallet(agentId) == payTo` in the 402 response. If\n   they differ, abort — someone is proxying my card with their own address.\n4. **Reputation aggregate.** `getSummary(agentId, [], \"\", \"\")` → `(count, value,\n   decimals)`. Free `eth_call`; no gas, no account. Filter by tag to ask narrower\n   questions (`getSummary(agentId, [], \"accuracy\", \"\")`).\n5. **Rater provenance.** `readAllFeedback` yields the rater addresses; each\n   feedback file carries `proofOfPayment.txHash`. Cross-check that hash against\n   USDC `Transfer` logs to `payTo`. A rating backed by a real $0.35 transfer costs\n   $0.35 to fake; a rating that isn't, costs nothing and should be discarded.\n\n**Sybil resistance is deliberately reader-side.** `giveFeedback` is permissionless\n— the contract only blocks the agent owner and its operators from rating\nthemselves. Anyone else can write anything. That is why `getSummary` and\n`readAllFeedback` take a `clientAddresses[]` filter: the honest aggregate is\nwhatever the *reader* computes over the raters *the reader* trusts. I cannot\ninflate my own score by curating the set, because I do not hold the filter. A\nclient with no opinion about who to trust should weight by proof-of-payment (check\n5) and by rater age.\n\n**Cold start, stated honestly.** At `count == 0` none of this proves quality —\nonly that I am a real, domain-bound, wallet-bound identity that has been around\nsince block N. That is a floor, not a recommendation. The intended bootstrap is\nthat $0.35 is small enough for a client to buy its own evidence: the price is\nitself the trust mechanism for the first call. `supportedTrust` therefore also\nadvertises `crypto-economic`, and §7 describes the validation path that upgrades\n\"they served me\" into \"they were right.\"\n\n---\n\n## 4. Payment — x402 `exact` on Base\n\n**Price.** $0.35 → USDC has 6 decimals → `maxAmountRequired: \"350000\"`, quoted as\na fixed atomic amount. Not a USD oracle lookup: USDC is the unit of account here,\nand a quote that moves between the 402 and the retry is a quote the client cannot\nsign.\n\n**The 402.** Unpaid requests get `402 Payment Required` with an `accepts[]` entry:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n  \"network\": \"base\",\n  \"maxAmountRequired\": \"350000\",\n  \"asset\": \"0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",  // native USDC on Base\n  \"payTo\": \"0x…\",\n  \"resource\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast?lat=…&lon=…\",\n  \"description\": \"Weather forecast, single point, up to 168h\",\n  \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n  \"outputSchema\": { /* … */ },\n  \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n  \"extra\": { \"name\": \"USD Coin\", \"version\": \"2\" }\n}\n```\n\n`extra` is not decoration — it is the EIP-712 domain (`name`, `version`) of the\nUSDC contract, which the client needs to build a signature the token will accept.\nBase mainnet USDC uses `\"USD Coin\"` / `\"2\"`; Base Sepolia's uses `\"USDC\"` / `\"2\"`.\nGetting this wrong produces signatures that verify locally and revert onchain.\n\n### 4.1 Why \"USDC but no ETH\" is not a problem\n\nThis is the crux, and it is EIP-3009's entire reason for existing.\n\nThe client never sends a transaction. It signs a **`TransferWithAuthorization`**\nEIP-712 struct — `from`, `to`, `value`, `validAfter`, `validBefore`, `nonce` —\nwhich is just bytes. Signing costs nothing and touches no chain. It base64s that\ninto `X-PAYMENT` and sends an ordinary HTTPS request.\n\nThe **facilitator** submits `transferWithAuthorization` to the USDC contract and\npays the ETH gas from its own balance. USDC moves directly from the client to my\n`payTo`. The client's ETH balance is zero at the start and zero at the end, and\nit holds no relationship with the facilitator either.\n\nThree properties fall out of this, all of which the constraints demand:\n- No pre-funding: the authorization is drawn against the client's own balance at\n  settlement time. I never hold client money.\n- No accounts: `nonce` is a random 32 bytes, not a sequence number, so\n  authorizations need no per-payer state on either side.\n- No invoices: the 402 *is* the invoice, and it lives for `maxTimeoutSeconds`.\n\n### 4.2 Server-side verification rules\n\nThe server must **never** hand the facilitator the requirements the client echoed\nback. It reconstructs its own `PaymentRequirements` from local config and the\nactual request URL, and checks, before spending a facilitator call:\n\n- `scheme == \"exact\"`, `network == \"base\"`\n- `authorization.to == payTo` (case-insensitive)\n- `BigInt(authorization.value) >= 350000n`\n- `validBefore` is far enough ahead to survive settlement; `validAfter` is in the past\n\nThen `POST /verify`, do the work, `POST /settle`. Verify-before-work avoids\nserving unpaid calls; settle-after-work avoids charging for a call that errored.\nThe window between them is the residual risk: if settlement fails after the\nforecast is computed, the client gets a 402 and I have burned compute. At $0.35\nagainst a cached forecast that is the correct trade.\n\n**Idempotency.** The EIP-3009 nonce is the natural request key. The server caches\n`nonce → response` so a client retrying a dropped connection gets the same body\nback instead of a second settlement attempt (which would revert as a used\nauthorization anyway, but would return a confusing 402). The cache also closes\nthe replay window on the read path.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Onchain dependencies\n\n| Dependency | Address / id | Used for | If it breaks |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Base L2 | `eip155:8453` | settlement, all registry state | Payments and ratings stall. Sequencer outage = service outage. |\n| USDC (native, Circle) | `0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913` | the money | Circle can freeze addresses; a frozen `payTo` stops all revenue. |\n| EIP-3009 on that contract | `transferWithAuthorization` | gasless payment | Not upgradeable away in practice, but it is a proxy — worth monitoring. |\n| ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry | `0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432` | discovery, identity, wallet binding | New clients cannot find me. Existing ones are unaffected. |\n| ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry | `0x8004BAa17C55a88189AE136b182e5fdA19dE9b63` | ratings | Trust signal goes stale; payments still work. |\n| ERC-8004 ValidationRegistry | canonical `0x8004Cb…` deployment (§7) | optional accuracy attestations | Optional path only. |\n| ERC-4337 EntryPoint + a USDC paymaster | e.g. Circle Paymaster on Base | lets an ETH-less client pay gas to *rate* me | Ratings become impossible for ETH-less clients — see §6.1. |\n\n**Note the registries are UUPS proxies** owned by the ERC-8004 deployer key. That\nis a real trust assumption, not a footnote: an upgrade could change registry\nsemantics under me. Mitigation is to snapshot `Registered` and `NewFeedback`\nevents locally so my identity and rating history remain provable from event logs\neven if the contract behaviour changes.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Offchain dependencies\n\n| Dependency | Role | Failure mode / mitigation |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| **x402 facilitator** (CDP `https://api.cdp.coinbase.com/platform/v2/x402`, or self-hosted) | `/verify`, `/settle`, pays gas | **The single most load-bearing offchain dependency.** If it is down, nobody can pay. Mitigate by running a fallback facilitator — it is just an RPC signer with an ETH balance and two endpoints. Config takes a list. |\n| **Base RPC provider** | reads registries, verifies proofs | Use two providers; disagreement is a signal, not a coin-flip. |\n| **DNS + ACME/TLS** | binds the name to the content | Expired cert breaks *discovery* (registration file unfetchable), not just transport. Alert well before renewal. |\n| **Weather data source** | the actual product | Rate limits and licence terms are mine to absorb. Cache aggressively; the same lat/lon/hour is one upstream call regardless of how many agents buy it. |\n| **IPFS pinning** (optional) | durable feedback files | Only used if a client asks me to mirror its rating. |\n| **Operator hot wallet** | one-time `register()`, `setAgentWallet` | Needs a little ETH once. Should not be the same key as `payTo`. |\n| **Receipt signing key** | signs receipts (§6.2) | Must be the agent wallet or a key published in the agent card, or receipts are unverifiable. |\n\nThe facilitator deserves emphasis: it sees every payment and could censor\nselectively. It cannot steal — the EIP-3009 authorization names `payTo` and\n`value`, and the signature covers both — but it can decline to broadcast. This is\nwhy facilitator choice is config, not a hardcoded constant.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Ratings\n\n### 7.1 The gas problem nobody mentions\n\n`giveFeedback` is an onchain write. The client that just paid me **has no ETH** —\nthat was the premise. So the naive \"client rates me onchain\" story is broken at\nstep one.\n\nTwo workable paths, both client-side, both of which I must support rather than\nassume away:\n\n- **ERC-4337 + a USDC paymaster** (Circle Paymaster is deployed on Base and takes\n  USDC for gas via an EIP-2612 permit). The client is a smart account; it submits\n  a UserOperation and pays gas in the same token it used to pay me. This is the\n  path I document in the agent card.\n- **A relayer.** Feedback is not authenticated to a specific submitter beyond\n  `msg.sender`, so a client without a smart account cannot delegate the write\n  without also delegating the identity that the rating is attributed to. This is\n  a genuine limitation of permissionless feedback: the rater must be able to\n  transact as itself.\n\nSo: an ETH-less client can *pay* me with no extra machinery, but must be a smart\naccount to *rate* me. Worth being explicit, because it means the rating loop has\na strictly higher client-side requirement than the payment loop.\n\n### 7.2 What the server does to enable it\n\nAfter settlement the server mints a **receipt** and returns `X-Receipt-Id`.\n`GET /v1/receipt/{id}` returns it, signed EIP-191 by the agent wallet:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"receiptId\": \"…\",\n  \"agentId\": 4242,\n  \"agentRegistry\": \"eip155:8453:0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432\",\n  \"endpoint\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast\",\n  \"payer\": \"0x…\",\n  \"amount\": \"350000\",\n  \"asset\": \"eip155:8453/erc20:0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",\n  \"txHash\": \"0x…\",          // the settlement transaction\n  \"requestHash\": \"0x…\",     // keccak of the canonical request\n  \"responseHash\": \"0x…\",    // keccak of the exact bytes served\n  \"servedAt\": \"2026-07-27T11:15:27Z\",\n  \"signature\": \"0x…\"        // EIP-191 over the above, by the agent wallet\n}\n```\n\nThe client turns that into an ERC-8004 feedback file — the spec's format, with\n`proofOfPayment` populated from `txHash` — hosts it (IPFS, its own domain, or\n`POST /v1/feedback` for me to mirror), and calls:\n\n```solidity\ngiveFeedback(\n  agentId,        // 4242\n  value,          // int128, e.g. 95\n  valueDecimals,  // 0\n  \"accuracy\",     // tag1\n  \"latency\",      // tag2\n  \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast\",  // endpoint\n  feedbackURI,\n  feedbackHash    // keccak256 of the file bytes\n);\n```\n\n`responseHash` is the part that makes a rating *contestable* rather than merely\nloud. It commits me to the exact bytes I served, so if a client rates me 5/100\nfor a bad forecast, I can `appendResponse` with the evidence — and equally, I\ncannot later claim I served something I did not. Mutual, which is the point.\n\n**I deliberately do not gate rating on holding a receipt.** The registry is\npermissionless; a receipt-gated design would be theatre, since clients can rate\nme with or without one. The receipt's job is to make *honest* ratings verifiable\nby third parties, not to suppress dishonest ones. Suppression is the reader's job\n(§3), and the reader is better placed to do it.\n\nMirroring at `POST /v1/feedback` is a convenience with an obvious conflict of\ninterest — I could refuse to host a bad review. So the endpoint stores\ncontent-addressed and returns the hash, the client verifies the hash before\ncommitting it onchain, and the design assumes serious clients use IPFS. The\nonchain `feedbackHash` means my copy can be checked for tampering by anyone; it\ncannot be silently edited, only withheld — at which point the missing file is\nitself a signal.\n\n### 7.3 Validation (optional, and the strongest signal available)\n\nReputation says *\"it answered me.\"* For a forecast service the interesting claim\nis *\"it was right\"* — and unusually, that is objectively checkable after the fact.\nThe ValidationRegistry supports exactly this: an independent validator agent\ncompares my past forecasts against observed weather and calls\n`validationResponse(requestHash, response /* 0-100 */, responseURI, responseHash,\ntag)`. Because my receipts commit to `responseHash`, a validator can prove what I\npredicted without my cooperation. `supportedTrust` advertises this. It is the one\nsignal in this system that cannot be bought.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Security notes\n\n- **Reconstruct requirements server-side.** The client's echoed\n  `paymentRequirements` are untrusted input; a facilitator asked to verify\n  attacker-supplied requirements will happily confirm a $0.000001 payment.\n- **`payTo` is not the hot wallet.** Payments accrue to an address whose key is\n  not on the web server.\n- **Nonce cache is the idempotency and replay boundary** — bound its size and TTL\n  to `maxTimeoutSeconds` plus settlement latency.\n- **Receipts are signed but not secret.** They reveal the payer and amount, both\n  already public onchain.\n- **`resource` in the 402 must be the real, fully-qualified request URL**,\n  otherwise a client signing for one endpoint could be replayed against another.\n- **Rate-limit the free surface** (`/.well-known/*`, the 402 itself) independently\n  of the paid path; the 402 is unauthenticated and cheap to spam.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Honest limitations\n\n1. **Cold start is unsolved and unsolvable by design** (§3). Low price is the\n   mitigation, not a fix.\n2. **Feedback is permissionless**, so a raw average is meaningless. Every claim\n   this design makes about reputation depends on the *reader* filtering.\n3. **The rating loop requires a smart-account client** (§7.1), a strictly higher\n   bar than the payment loop.\n4. **The facilitator is a censorship chokepoint** — mitigated by making it\n   swappable, not eliminated.\n5. **ERC-8004 registries are upgradeable proxies** under a third-party owner.\n6. **ERC-8004 is a Draft ERC.** Addresses and signatures here are verified against\n   the July 2026 contracts repo; both can move before Final, and the registration\n   file `type` URI is version-pinned for that reason.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4505,"cache_read_input_tokens":53071,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4505},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"dfbd3ef5-7f4a-4b16-8c7c-a2a0c0722761","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:26:01.420Z","request_id":"req_011CdSUe57FggjPg7BF4FB5c"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016GgdKo8CLMAE5JTHA345dH","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace/design.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7771f28f-4ac8-40d8-8901-5e476f40630b","uuid":"2569e030-6ff1-40ea-9103-784a8e3fb716","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:26:01.453Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111527Z-claude-no-skill-2/workspace/design.md","content":"# forecast.example.com — service-side design\n\nSells weather forecasts to autonomous agents. **$0.35/call, USDC on Base, no\naccounts, no humans.**\n\nNothing here is invented protocol. The service is the intersection of two\nexisting standards plus DNS:\n\n| Need | Standard | Why it is the answer |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Per-call payment, no accounts | **x402** (`exact` scheme, HTTP transport) | Payment is a property of the request, not of a relationship. |\n| Paying with USDC and zero ETH | **EIP-3009** `transferWithAuthorization` | Caller signs; the facilitator broadcasts and pays gas. |\n| Discovery by a stranger, no catalog I own | **ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry** on Base | A neutral onchain index. I can write to it; I cannot control who reads it or gatekeep who else registers. |\n| Ratings other agents can act on | **ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry** on Base | Feedback is onchain, permissionless, and readable before the first call. |\n| Machine-readable trust judgement | Registration file + bidirectional domain binding + onchain aggregates | Everything a human would read from docs, published as JSON at fixed paths. |\n\nVerified against the normative sources (July 2026):\n`x402-foundation/x402` `specs/x402-specification-v1.md`, `specs/transports-v1/http.md`,\n`specs/extensions/bazaar.md`; `erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts` `ERC8004SPEC.md` and\n`ReputationRegistryUpgradeable.sol`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-end flow\n\nNo step below involves a human, an account, or an API key.\n\n```\n  ┌── discovery (cold, stranger) ─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │ 1. client scans IdentityRegistry `Registered` events on Base          │\n  │    (or any third-party indexer of them — none of which I operate)     │\n  │ 2. reads tokenURI(agentId) -> https://forecast.example.com/           │\n  │      .well-known/agent-registration.json                              │\n  │ 3. fetches that file: skills, price, endpoint, supportedTrust         │\n  │ 4. checks the file's registrations[] points back at (registry,agentId)│\n  │      -> domain <-> onchain identity are bound both ways               │\n  │ 5. ReputationRegistry.getSummary(agentId, [], \"\", \"\") -> score, count │\n  │    readAllFeedback(...) -> individual raters, filtered client-side    │\n  │ 6. spot-checks feedback files' proofOfPayment.txHash against USDC     │\n  │      Transfer logs to payTo  -> raters really were customers          │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │ decides to call\n  ┌── payment ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │ 7. GET /v1/forecast?lat&lon           -> 402 + accepts[] + bazaar ext │\n  │ 8. client signs EIP-3009 authorization (offline, no gas, no ETH)      │\n  │ 9. GET again with X-PAYMENT: base64(PaymentPayload)                   │\n  │10. server -> facilitator POST /verify  (server's OWN requirements)    │\n  │11. server computes the forecast                                       │\n  │12. server -> facilitator POST /settle  -> facilitator pays the gas    │\n  │13. 200 + body + X-PAYMENT-RESPONSE + X-Receipt-Id                     │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n  ┌── rating ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n  │14. GET /v1/receipt/{id} -> signed receipt incl. settlement txHash     │\n  │15. client builds ERC-8004 feedback file (embeds proofOfPayment)       │\n  │    hosts it on IPFS, or POST /v1/feedback to have us mirror it        │\n  │16. client calls ReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(agentId, ...)         │\n  │    gas paid in USDC via ERC-4337 paymaster (see §6.1)                 │\n  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Discovery — findable by an agent that has never heard of me\n\nThe constraint is *\"without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\"* That rules\nout my own directory, my own well-known index, and my own search API. It does\n**not** rule out a neutral registry that I merely write into on the same terms as\neveryone else.\n\n**Primary channel: ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry on Base**\n(`0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432`). It is an ERC-721 where each agent\nis a token and `tokenURI` is the registration file. A stranger discovers me by\nreading `Registered(agentId, agentURI, owner)` logs — from their own node, a\npublic RPC, or any third-party indexer. I cannot delist a competitor, rank\nmyself higher, or deny anyone read access. The registry is the catalog, and it\nis nobody's property.\n\n**Secondary channel: the x402 Bazaar.** My 402 response carries the `bazaar`\nextension, which declares the HTTP method, input params, and an output example\nplus JSON Schema.
